# Definition for a binary tree node.
# class TreeNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, left=None, right=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.left = left
#         self.right = right
class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue=deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst=[]
        while queue:
            levels=[]
            for i in range(len(queue)+1): 
                tmp=queue.popleft()
                if tmp:
                    levels.append(tmp.val)
                    queue.append(tmp.right)  
                    queue.append(tmp.left)   

            if levels:
                lst.add(levels)
        return lst[::-1]

- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.

from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst = []
        while queue:
            levels = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                if node:
                    levels.append(node.val)
                    queue.append(node.left)
                    queue.append(node.right)
            if levels:
                lst.append(levels)
        return lst[::-1]

from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque([root])
        result = []
        while queue:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                if node:
                    level.append(node.val)
                    queue.append(node.left)
                    queue.append(node.right)
            if level:
                result.append(level)
        return result[::-1]

```
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst = []
        while queue:
            levels = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                tmp = queue.popleft()
                if tmp:
                    levels.append(tmp.val)
                    queue.append(tmp.left)
                    queue.append(tmp.right)
            if levels:
                lst.append(levels)
        return lst[::-1]
```

Let's consider if the code should be exactly:
```
class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []
        q = deque([root])
        res = []
        while q:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                node = q.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    q.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    q.append(node.right)
            res.append(level)
        return res[::-1]
```
This is a common clean solution. It avoids None in queue and `if node` check. It also handles root None early. It is not a broad rewrite; it's a standard fix. Which is better? Let's maybe produce final code as:
```
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst = []
        while queue:
            levels = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                tmp = queue.popleft()
                if tmp:
                    levels.append(tmp.val)
                    queue.append(tmp.left)
                    queue.append(tmp.right)
            if levels:
                lst.append(levels)
        return lst[::-1]
```
This is final. Need no explanation.

from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst = []
        while queue:
            levels = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                tmp = queue.popleft()
                if tmp:
                    levels.append(tmp.val)
                    queue.append(tmp.left)
                    queue.append(tmp.right)
            if levels:
                lst.append(levels)
        return lst[::-1]

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst = []

        while queue:
            levels = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                tmp = queue.popleft()
                if tmp:
                    levels.append(tmp.val)
                    queue.append(tmp.left)
                    queue.append(tmp.right)

            if levels:
                lst.append(levels)

        return lst[::-1]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst = []

        while queue:
            levels = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                tmp = queue.popleft()
                if tmp:
                    levels.append(tmp.val)
                    queue.append(tmp.left)
                    queue.append(tmp.right)

            if levels:
                lst.append(levels)

        return lst[::-1]
